# Third-Party Notices

Capin includes open-source third-party components. This notice is
included in the Windows packages and in the macOS app bundle.

The list covers the components used by the Windows and macOS binaries
(529 packages in the union of all four shipped targets).
A package may be present on only some platforms or architectures.

Generated from Cargo.lock. Dependency-set fingerprint: e7ea260284dede6f7021029fb1e0efabc99946e775c19540866f0a3607e801fa.

License expressions are shown as declared by the upstream package (usually
SPDX syntax). Where a component is offered under several licenses, Capin
relies on one of the permissive options; the full upstream expression is
shown for transparency. License
texts and copyright notices collected from the crate sources or their
upstream repositories are reproduced below. The upstream license remains
authoritative.

<pre>
# License

The licensing in this repository is a bit complicated:
- The crates `objc2`, `block2`, `objc2-foundation` and `objc2-encode` are
  [currently][#23] licensed under [the MIT license][MIT].
- All other crates are trio-licensed under the [Zlib], [Apache-2.0] or [MIT]
  license, at your option.
- Some example code may be more restrictively licensed, see each example for
  details.

Furthermore, the crates are (usually automatically) derived from Apple SDKs,
and that may have implications for licensing, see below for details.

[#23]: https://github.com/madsmtm/objc2/issues/23
[MIT]: ./LICENSE-MIT.txt
[Zlib]: ./LICENSE-ZLIB.txt
[Apache-2.0]: ./LICENSE-APACHE.txt


## Apple SDKs

These crates are derived from Apple SDKs shipped with Xcode. You can obtain a
copy of the Xcode license at:

https://www.apple.com/legal/sla/docs/xcode.pdf

Or by typing `xcodebuild -license` in your terminal.

From reading the license, it is unclear whether distributing derived works
such as these crates are allowed?

But in any case, to practically use these crates, you will have to link, and
that only works when you have the correct Xcode SDK available to provide the
required `.tbd` files, which is why we choose to still use the normal SPDX
identifiers in the crates (Xcode is required to use the crates, and when using
Xcode you have already agreed to the Xcode license).
</pre>

## Components whose license files were not present in the packaged crate

The following components declare the licenses in the table but their
crate packages do not carry a license file and no copyright override
was available. Their copyright notices are retained in the upstream
repositories linked below; the corresponding standard license texts
are included in the next section.

| Package | Version | License | Upstream source |
|---|---|---|---|
| geo | 0.33.1 | MIT OR Apache-2.0 | https://github.com/georust/geo |
| harfrust | 0.5.2 | MIT | https://github.com/harfbuzz/harfrust |
| i_key_sort | 0.10.3 | MIT | https://github.com/iShape-Rust/iKeySort |
| i_overlay | 4.5.2 | MIT OR Apache-2.0 | https://github.com/iShape-Rust/iOverlay |
| rstar | 0.12.2 | MIT OR Apache-2.0 | https://github.com/georust/rstar |

## Standard license texts

These texts cover components whose packaged crate did not include a
license file. They are the canonical SPDX texts for the licenses used by
this release.

The following license texts could not be bundled automatically; see
the SPDX license list:

- Apache-2.0: https://spdx.org/licenses/Apache-2.0.html

## Notes

- This notice is generated from the dependency graph and license files;
  it is not legal advice.
- Capin itself is licensed under the MIT License; see `LICENSE`.
- If a component or notice is missing, please open an issue in the
  Capin repository.

## Bundled OCR models

Capin bundles the PaddleOCR PP-OCRv6 tiny detection and recognition models
(`PP-OCRv6_tiny_det.onnx`, `PP-OCRv6_tiny_rec.onnx`) together with the
recognition dictionary (`ppocrv6_dict.txt`). They are distributed under the
Apache License 2.0; the full license text is reproduced in the Standard license
texts section below. The models are used only by the Windows x64/ARM64 OCR
backend and run locally through ONNX Runtime. Sources:
https://huggingface.co/PaddlePaddle/PP-OCRv6_tiny_det_onnx and
https://huggingface.co/PaddlePaddle/PP-OCRv6_tiny_rec_onnx.
